An apt `DPkg::Post-Invoke` hook restarts Chrome when the binary is newer than the running one (`/run/chrome/started`), so an upgrade never leaves it on replaced files; on EC2 the repository is in unattended-upgrades (`origin=Google LLC,suite=stable`). `winstond` may run exactly `systemctl restart chrome.service` as root (`/etc/sudoers.d/winstond-chrome`).
    - **The AMI** (`ami-067bdcdcc6577e9e2`, 2026-10-02) passed the build's check that Chrome starts with its sandbox on the EC2 kernel (Ubuntu's AppArmor allows Chrome's user namespaces). Production VMs get Chrome when they're next replaced, as by a restore.
    - **The local container** gets `ShmSize` 1 GB (Docker's 64 MB default crashes tabs) and `seccomp=unconfined`: Docker's default seccomp profile blocks the namespaces Chrome's sandbox uses ("Failed to move to new namespace … Operation not permitted"), and lifting it for the local stand-in keeps the sandbox on rather than running with `--no-sandbox`. Existing local VMs pick it up when they next roll onto a new image (or with `bun run vm:reset`).
    - **Checked locally (arm64):** Chrome answered on CDP with its profile on the volume; `kill -9` was restarted by systemd in seconds; a frozen Chrome (`SIGSTOP`) was restarted by `winstond` after 65 s; the profile survived a container restart. **Memory, 5 heavy sites** (YouTube, CNN, Amazon, NYT, Google Maps) plus a blank tab: 1.69 GB settled, 1.99 GB peak (the cgroup's figure, which includes page cache), so it fits a 4 GB `t3a.medium` with its 2 GB swap. More parallel agent windows would meet the limit, which restarts Chrome, not the VM; revisit the size if that shows up in use.
  - **Rolling VMs onto a new image (eadb89):** CI's `ami` job builds the AMI whenever a push to `main` touches `image/` (after `check`; the manual AMI workflow stays for rebuilds), recording it in `/winston/vm-ami`. Each VM records the image its instance was built from (`vms.image_id`: the instance's AMI on EC2, the image id locally). Every 15 minutes agents compares them with the current image (`VmProvider.currentImage`: the SSM parameter on EC2, the local image's id in Docker) and queues `roll_vm` for each ready VM on another image, or none recorded, whose user is in their rollout hours (`VM_ROLLOUT_HOURS`; 3–5 am in their time zone on EC2, any time locally) with no run in progress and no live handoff from the last day. The job checks all that again, then replaces the instance (`provisionVm` with `replace`: a new instance from the current image, the same live data volume, a new registration token; not a snapshot restore). A busy VM waits for the next sweep; a computer is offline for the few minutes the swap takes. `bun run prod vm:roll <email>` moves one now, at any hour (still not while it's busy).
  - **Formatting:** `packer fmt` is part of `bun run format` / `format:check`.

## 19. CDK stacks (`infra/`)

| Stack      | Contents                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Network`  | VPC across 2 AZs: public subnets (Fargate tasks and VMs with public IPs, so no NAT) and isolated subnets (RDS)                                                            |
| `Data`     | RDS Postgres (single-AZ, `db.t4g.micro` to start), KMS keys (tokens, binary signing), S3 buckets (`artifacts`: images and binaries. `blobs`: screenshots and attachments) |
| `Services` | ECS cluster, 4 Fargate services (`api`, `web`, `agents`, `gateway`), ALB with host-based routing (`api.`, `gateway.`), Secrets Manager secrets, per-service IAM roles     |
| `Edge`     | The ACM certificate (validated through Cloudflare DNS). DNS records live in Cloudflare, not CDK                                                                           |
| `Vm`       | Launch template, instance profile (SSM only), Data Lifecycle Manager snapshot policy, the backend's EC2 policy                                                            |
| `Ci`       | ECR repositories, GitHub OIDC provider + deploy role, the Terraform state bucket for `infra/gcp`                                                                          |
| `Budget`   | AWS Budgets alerts (see §8)                                                                                                                                               |

- GCP resources live separately in `infra/gcp` (Terraform).
- **Network (built):** a VPC (`10.0.0.0/16`) across `us-east-1a` and `us-east-1b`, both of which offer `t3a.medium` and `t4g.micro`. Each zone has a `/24` public subnet and a `/24` isolated subnet, with **no NAT gateway**, so nothing bills by the hour or the gigabyte.
  - **Security groups** (all defined here so other stacks only reference them):
    - `alb`: 443 from anywhere (IPv4 and IPv6).
    - `api`, `web` and `gateway`: their own port from `alb`; `gateway` also takes 3001 from `agents`, for the internal API.
    - `agents` and `ops` (one-off admin tasks): no inbound.
    - `database`: 5432 from the four services and `ops` only, and no outbound.
    - `vm`: **no inbound rules at all**.
  - **Ports** are the same as local (`api` 3000, `gateway` 3001, `web` 3002). The gateway serves the VM websocket and the internal API on one port, so the load balancer must forward only the websocket path, never `/internal`.
  - **VPC endpoints:** an S3 gateway endpoint (free; VMs download binaries and services read blobs without leaving AWS's network). No interface endpoints for Secrets Manager or KMS: they cost about $7/month each per zone, and tasks reach those APIs over their public IPs with TLS and IAM anyway.
  - Stack tests check no NAT gateway, two zones, no ingress on `vm`, `agents` and `ops`, Postgres reachable only from the services and `ops` with no route out, and the load balancer taking only HTTPS.
- **Data (built):** everything stateful, in a stack with termination protection, and every database, key and bucket is retained if it's ever removed from the code.
  - **Postgres:** RDS for PostgreSQL 18.6 (the same as local), `db.t4g.micro`, single-AZ, in the isolated subnets behind the `database` security group, not publicly accessible. 20 GB of gp3, growing automatically up to 100 GB. Encrypted, 7 days of automated backups, deletion protection on. Backups at 07:00 UTC and maintenance on Sundays at 07:30 UTC (the early hours in New York). The database is `winston`; the master user is `postgres`, with its password managed and rotated by RDS (§12a). About $14/month.
  - **KMS keys** (about $1/month each): `alias/winston/tokens`, symmetric with yearly automatic rotation, for the token vault; and `alias/winston/vm-binary-signing`, an asymmetric **ECC P-256** key for `ECDSA_SHA_256` signatures, which VMs verify offline with WebCrypto. The key spec can't change after creation. Key policies keep CDK's default (the account, which delegates to IAM), and each role gets access through its own IAM policy, so a key policy never names `*` or another account.
  - **S3 buckets** (generated names): `artifacts` (VM binaries and signatures, versioned, with old versions expiring after 90 days) and `blobs` (content-addressed screenshots and attachments, never expiring, since they're part of the record). Both block all public access, use S3-managed encryption, disable ACLs, deny non-TLS requests, and abort incomplete uploads after a day.
  - Stack tests check termination protection, retain policies, the database's settings, bucket protections and that key policies only name the account.
- **Ci (built):** one ECR repository per image (the four services and `ops`), `winston/<image>`, with immutable tags (a tag is a commit SHA and never changes meaning), scan on push, and the last 30 images kept for rollbacks. They live here rather than in Services so images can be pushed before the first deploy of a service that runs them.
  - **GitHub's OIDC provider** (`token.actions.githubusercontent.com`, audience `sts.amazonaws.com`, native `AWS::IAM::OIDCProvider`) and two roles trusted **only** for `repo:nikolasgioannou@48188665/winston@1390141974:ref:refs/heads/main`; no AWS keys are stored in GitHub. The repository uses GitHub's **immutable subject** format, with the owner's and repository's numeric ids, so a renamed or re-created repository can't match. (The first deploy failed on the older `repo:<owner>/<repo>` form.)
  - **`winston-github-deploy`** (`ci.yml`'s deploy job) holds only what the workflow does itself: push and pull the `winston/*` repositories (plus `ecr:GetAuthorizationToken`), set and read `/winston/image-tag`, describe the `winston-*` stacks, run the `winston-ops` task and pass it the Services stack's roles (`iam:PassedToService` = ECS), read `/winston/ops` logs, upload under `vm/*` in the artifacts bucket, `kms:Sign` with the signing key, and **assume CDK's bootstrap roles** (`cdk-hnb659fds-*`), through which every CloudFormation change goes. Those roles trust the account, so this is how `cdk deploy` works without granting the deploy role CloudFormation or IAM itself. The ops task's family and log group are named (`winston-ops`, `/winston/ops`) so these grants need no reference to the Services stack, which deploys after this one.
  - **`winston-github-ami`** (`ami.yml`) holds Packer's documented EC2 permissions for `amazon-ebs` and writing `/winston/vm-ami`, nothing else.
  - Stack tests check the trust conditions, that the deploy role has no wildcard actions and uses `*` resources only for describes and the ECR login, that it can't create or update stacks itself, and that the AMI role is EC2 plus the one parameter.
- **Services (built):** an ECS cluster in the public subnets, a Cloud Map private DNS namespace `winston.internal`, and four Fargate services on **ARM64** (Graviton is about 20% cheaper, and the distroless Bun image is multi-arch).
  - **Sizes:** `api`, `gateway` and `web` at 0.25 vCPU / 512 MB, `agents` at 0.5 vCPU / 1 GB. Roughly $7/month per small task and $14 for agents, plus $3.65 per task's public IPv4; the load balancer about $17 plus usage. With everything running, about $70/month before RDS.
  - **Images:** every service runs the same tag, read from the SSM parameter **`/winston/image-tag`** through a CloudFormation parameter, which CloudFormation re-resolves on every deploy. A deploy pushes the images, sets the parameter, then deploys; deploying any other stack leaves the services alone. The parameter isn't in CDK (a deploy owns it).
  - **Task definitions:** each gets its own secrets (`Secrets.environmentFor`), `DATABASE_URL` without a password plus `DATABASE_SECRET_ARN` (§12a), `*_HOST=0.0.0.0`, `LOG_LEVEL=info`, and a 1-month log group. `gateway`, `web` and `agents` get `TOKEN_KMS_KEY_ID`. The rest of each service's environment is in `infra/src/services.ts`; for example `agents` also gets `BLOB_BUCKET`, `GATEWAY_INTERNAL_URL=http://gateway.winston.internal:3001`, `VM_GATEWAY_URL=wss://gateway.runwinston.com`, `VM_PROVIDER=ec2` and the launch template and subnets, and the gateway gets `ARTIFACTS_BUCKET`.
  - **IAM (task roles):** every service may read the database secret. The gateway may read the `artifacts` bucket (the VM manifest, and presigning binary downloads). `api` and `agents` get `kms:Decrypt` and `kms:GenerateDataKey` on the tokens key, the gateway only `kms:Decrypt` (it uses connections' tokens for mail and calendar calls), and `web` only `kms:GenerateDataKey`. `api` has the grant but doesn't read tokens today (no token vault, no `TOKEN_KMS_KEY_ID`). Only `agents` may get, put and delete in the `blobs` bucket. The execution roles read each service's own secrets.
  - **Deployments:** rolling (100% minimum, 200% maximum) with the **deployment circuit breaker and automatic rollback**.
  - **Load balancer:** internet-facing, HTTPS only on 443 with the Edge certificate and AWS's recommended TLS policy, default action a plain 404. Rules route `api.runwinston.com` to `api`, `gateway.runwinston.com` **and only the paths `/vm/connect` and `/handoff/connect`** to `gateway` (the internal API is never routed), and `runwinston.com` to `web` as CloudFront's origin. Target groups health-check `/health` every 15 s and drain for 30 s. The idle timeout stays at 60 s, since VMs ping every 20 s.
  - **agents → gateway** goes through Cloud Map: the gateway registers as `gateway.winston.internal` (an A record per task), and the security groups allow only agents on 3001. Service Connect was rejected (an Envoy sidecar per task for one internal call) and so was an internal load balancer (another ~$17/month). There's one gateway task, but during a deploy the old and new overlap for a couple of minutes and only one holds a given VM's socket. So **calls are routed by VM**: a gateway that takes a VM's socket writes its own address to `vms.gateway_url` (`GATEWAY_ADVERTISE_URL`, else the task's private IPv4 and port, skipping link-local addresses: Fargate's task metadata interface is `169.254.172.2`, which nothing else reaches; the address is logged at startup) and clears it when the socket closes and it holds no other; the agents' gateway client sends each user's calls there, falling back to the Cloud Map name when none is recorded or the recorded one can't be reached. It also retries for up to 30 s, counted from the first failure (so a long command cut off near its end gets the whole window), while the gateway answers `409 vm_unavailable` or can't be reached. That covers the VM's socket moving: the ALB cuts it when the old gateway task finishes draining, the old gateway fails the commands that were on it (§15), and the retry finds the VM on the new one. `winstond`'s own restart after a self-update happens only when it's idle (§10). Before this, Winston's commands failed with "Your computer isn't reachable" through every deploy.
  - **What runs today:** `desiredCounts` in `infra/src/app.ts`. All four run. Until production keys are set, the bot token and Google client secrets hold well-formed placeholders, so `api` and `web` start and pass their health checks but can't talk to Telegram or Google. `agents` runs too, with the EC2 VM provider. `bun run prod:keys` replaces the placeholders with the real keys ([runbooks/secrets.md](runbooks/secrets.md)).
  - **CloudFront** (`runwinston.com`) lives in this stack, next to its origin; in Edge it would make a cycle, since the load balancer uses Edge's certificate. Pages and server functions use AWS's `CachingDisabled` policy with `AllViewer` (cookies, query strings and the viewer's `Host` all reach the origin, so the load balancer routes by host and CloudFront checks the origin's TLS against `runwinston.com`); `/assets/*` uses `CachingOptimized` with only `Host` forwarded, honouring the origin's year-long `immutable` header. HTTPS only (HTTP redirects), TLS 1.2+, HTTP/2 and HTTP/3, price class 100 (North America and Europe).
    - **The origin only answers CloudFront:** CloudFront adds `X-Winston-Origin` with a generated secret (`winston/cloudfront-origin-header`, read through a CloudFormation dynamic reference), and the load balancer forwards `runwinston.com` to `web` only when the header matches; anything else gets the default 404. A security group can't do it, since the load balancer also serves `api` and `gateway` to everyone.
    - **No `www`:** the certificate doesn't cover it, and adding it means a replacement certificate and new validation records. Nobody types `www` for a friends-only app; it can come back with a certificate change.
  - **The `ops` task:** a task definition (0.25 vCPU / 512 MB, ARM64) on the `ops` image, with its own security group (no inbound; Postgres allows it) and read access to the database secret, no other secrets. `bun run prod` starts it as a one-off task for migrations and admin commands ([runbooks/production.md](runbooks/production.md)); the stack outputs the cluster, task family, subnets and security group it needs.
  - Stack tests check that each task gets exactly its mapped secrets and never a database password, the circuit breaker, ARM64 and public IPs, an HTTPS-only listener with three rules, that `/internal` is never routed, and who may use the tokens key and the blobs bucket.
- **Edge (certificate built):** one ACM certificate in `us-east-1` for `runwinston.com`, `api.runwinston.com` and `gateway.runwinston.com`, used by both CloudFront and the load balancer. It's validated by CNAME records added by hand in Cloudflare, and stacks output the targets for the site's and API's records, which are added by hand too ([runbooks/dns.md](runbooks/dns.md)). The founder chose hand-added records over a script with a Cloudflare API token.
- **Vm (built):** what surrounds users' VMs; `agents` creates the VMs themselves (§10).
  - **Launch template `winston-vm`:** `t3a.medium`, AMI resolved at each launch from `/winston/vm-ami` (`resolve:ssm:`, so a new AMI needs no deploy), the zero-inbound `vm` security group (the public subnets give a public IP), an encrypted 12 GB gp3 root, **IMDSv2 required with a hop limit of 1**, and tags on what each launch creates (`winston:role=vm` on the instance, `root` on its root volume).
  - **Instance role:** `AmazonSSMManagedInstanceCore` (Session Manager), and nothing else. Self-updates download through presigned URLs the gateway hands out.
  - **Snapshots:** a Data Lifecycle Manager policy snapshots every volume tagged `winston:role=data` nightly at 07:00 UTC and keeps **14** (two weeks to notice a problem; snapshots are incremental, so cheap). Snapshots copy the volume's tags, which is how the backend may delete them.
  - **Auto-recovery:** nothing to build. EC2's simplified automatic recovery is on by default for `t3a` (EBS-only, default tenancy), so no CloudWatch recover alarm.
  - **The backend's policy** (attached to `agents`' task role): `RunInstances` only with `ec2:LaunchTemplate` equal to this template; `CreateVolume` only with the request tag `winston:role=data`; `CreateTags` only during `RunInstances`/`CreateVolume`; start, stop, terminate and attach only on instances tagged `vm`; attach, detach and delete only volumes tagged `data`; delete only snapshots tagged `data`, and create volumes only from them (restores); read `/winston/vm-ami` (EC2 resolves the template's AMI as the caller); pass the instance role; and the describe calls, which have no resource-level permissions. Checked with IAM's policy simulator: launching from another template, creating or deleting a volume with another role tag, terminating an untagged instance and tagging after the fact are all denied.
  - Stack tests check IMDSv2 and the AMI parameter, that the instance role has only Session Manager, the backend policy's conditions, and the snapshot schedule.
- **Budget (built):** one AWS Budget, `winston-monthly` ($150 monthly cost limit), emailing when actual costs pass **$150** and when the forecast passes **$200** (both absolute thresholds). The address comes from the SSM parameter `/winston/alert-email` through a CloudFormation parameter, so it isn't in this public repository. OpenRouter's credit limit and low-balance alert are set on its own site ([runbooks/costs.md](runbooks/costs.md)).
- **The app (`infra/`, workspace `@winston/infra`):** CDK v2 in TypeScript, run directly by Bun (`cdk.json` `app: "bun bin/app.ts"`, no build step). The CLI (`aws-cdk`) is a dev dependency that runs under Node, and the app under Bun. Both are pinned exactly and bumped together: the CLI is versioned separately from `aws-cdk-lib`, and an older CLI can't read a newer library's output.
  - `src/app.ts` defines every stack with an explicit environment (`production`: account `766577085959`, `us-east-1`, `runwinston.com`), named `winston-<stack>`. A stack stays empty until its ticket fills it; CDK skips deploying empty stacks.
  - **Stateful resources can't be deleted by accident:** the `Data` stack has termination protection, and its resources get explicit retain policies and deletion protection as they're added.
  - **Cross-stack references are strong** (`@aws-cdk/core:defaultCrossStackReferences: strong`: CloudFormation exports and `Fn::ImportValue`). The app started on `weak` (`Fn::GetStackOutput`, CDK's recommendation since 2.254) and switched on 2026-10-01 after it caused a production outage: editing the database security group's description replaced the group, and the Data stack's RDS instance kept the old one, whose rules were gone, because **CloudFormation doesn't re-resolve `Fn::GetStackOutput` in a consumer whose template didn't change** (not even with `cdk deploy --force`). With strong references the same edit fails the producer's deploy ("export in use"), which is the failure we want. The cost is the "deadly embrace": removing a consumer's use of a value can block the producer's deploy; deploy the consumer first, or keep the export alive for one deploy with `stack.exportValue()`.
  - **Don't replace a referenced resource casually:** a security group's description, for one, can't change in place (`infra/src/network.ts` says so where the groups are made).
  - `cdk.json` sets every recommended feature flag, so behaviour doesn't drift when CDK adds new defaults, and turns off the CLI's telemetry (`cli-telemetry: false`). It keeps CDK's version reporting, the `CDKMetadata` resource that also keeps empty stacks valid. `cdk.context.json` is committed if lookups ever create it; `cdk.out/` is ignored.
  - **Synth includes CloudFormation validation** (`@aws-cdk/core:validateAgainstDefaultRules`), and `infra/src/app.test.ts` synthesizes the whole app with `cdk.json`'s flags inside the normal test suite, so broken infrastructure fails the pre-commit hook.
  - Root scripts `infra:synth`, `infra:diff` and `infra:deploy` (diff and deploy cover all stacks) need AWS credentials for diff and deploy: `AWS_PROFILE=winston-prod` locally (docs/runbooks/aws-access.md). The account is bootstrapped once with `cdk bootstrap`.

## 20. Website pages & states

**Layout:** public pages stand alone. Everything behind sign-in uses one **app shell with a sidebar**, grouped by purpose. There's no catch-all "settings" page. On mobile the sidebar collapses into a drawer.

- **App shell as built** (`apps/web/src/components/app-shell.tsx`, rendered by the `_authed` layout route): the resizable `Sidebar` from 640px up; below that, a top bar whose menu button opens the same navigation in `SidebarDrawer` (modal: it traps focus and locks scrolling). The sidebar is a flat list with no section headers for now: Home, Connected accounts (a blocks icon, covering every kind of account) and Profile. Telegram, sign-out and account deletion all live on Profile (the founder's call; Telegram is how you talk to Winston, part of your own setup rather than a connected data source). Items are real links; the current one is the longest item the path is at or under (so `/accounts/<id>` highlights Accounts). Nothing is pinned to the bottom: signing out and deleting the account live on `/profile` (the founder's call), whose placeholder already has a working **Sign out**. The drawer counts as open only on the page it was opened on, so any navigation closes it.
- **Routes:** the `_authed` layout's `beforeLoad` gets the user and the saved sidebar width in one server call (`getShellState`), redirecting to `/` (the sign-in page) without a user. Signed-in visitors to `/` go to `/home`. Pages not built yet render `PlaceholderPage`.
- **Home as built** (`src/pages/home-page.tsx`, `routes/_authed/home.tsx`): one loader, `getHomeState`, returns a `HomeState` (`src/server/home-state.ts`): the first name, the computer's status, whether Telegram is linked, how many accounts are connected (0 until the connections ticket) and a list of attention items (empty until the token lifecycle feeds it). The page is a pure function of it, so later tickets add their parts by filling in fields.
  - **Setup checklist** until the computer is ready (or `unreachable`, which only follows ready), Telegram is linked and an account is connected: "Welcome, <name>" and a card of three numbered steps. Each has a marker (number, spinner, check or cross), a line of explanation and its action on the right: Retry for a failed computer, Connect (with the QR code under it on wider screens) for Telegram, and Connect (to `/accounts`) for accounts. On phones the action drops below the text.
  - **Status summary** once all three are done: "Hi, <name>", any attention items as callouts with an action (e.g. Reconnect), then a Status card with the computer (Ready, or Not responding when `unreachable`), Telegram and accounts.
  - While the computer is setting up or Telegram isn't linked, the page re-runs its loader every 3 s. Retry calls `retryComputer`, then reloads; a failure shows a toast.
- **Accounts as built** (`src/pages/accounts-page.tsx`): the user's connections as DTOs, oldest first, in a card of setting rows laid out like Profile's channels: the provider icon, the provider ("Gmail") with a type badge ("Mail"), the address under it, a status badge (Connected, Expires soon, Expired, Disconnected) and an ⋯ menu with **Manage permissions** (the account dialog), **Reconnect** and **Disconnect** (after a confirmation). **Add account** opens a dialog (`Dialog` in `packages/ui`) listing what can be connected, grouped by type (Mail: Gmail; Calendar: Google Calendar), from one catalog (`connectableProviders` in `src/components/providers.tsx`), so a new provider is one entry. The empty state offers the same dialog. After connecting, a toast says which account was connected, or what went wrong. `/home` counts connections that aren't disconnected, and lists expired and then expiring ones as attention items with Reconnect.
- **One account as built** (`src/pages/account-dialog.tsx`, the founder's call: a dialog over the list rather than its own page): clicking an account opens it at `/accounts?account=<id>`, so it can be linked to; closing it returns to `/accounts`, and an id that isn't the user's simply doesn't open. The dialog has the provider's icon in a tile beside the title, the address as its title with the provider and a type badge under it, a callout with **Reconnect** unless the status is `ok`, then the permissions in a card. Reconnecting and disconnecting live in the row's ⋯ menu.
  - **Permissions:** one switch per capability of the domain, saving immediately (`setCapability`, which merges into the jsonb map that always names every capability, exactly what M5 enforces). A switch flips at once and saves quietly (no saving text, the founder's call); a failed save flips it back with a toast. A capability whose scope wasn't granted (`capabilityScopes` in `@winston/domain/connections`) shows Reconnect instead of a switch. With today's scopes that can't happen, since each domain's capabilities all need its essential scope, but the mapping keeps it right if scopes split. A disconnected account's switches are disabled.
  - **Disconnect** (from the row's menu, after a confirmation): `disconnectConnection` marks it `disconnected` at once, records `system.app.disconnected`, and queues `revoke_connection_token`, and in the same transaction ends the triggers tied to the connection (§3, System). The site can't decrypt, so the job runs in `agents`: it locks the row, and if the connection is still disconnected, revokes the grant with Google (`oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke`; an already invalid token counts as revoked) unless another live connection uses the same Google account, since revoking one grant revokes them all and would break it. Either way it then deletes the token. Reconnecting a disconnected account records `system.app.connected` again.
- **Profile as built** (`src/pages/profile-page.tsx`): three cards (the founder's layout). **You**: first name, last name and email as setting rows, each label on the left and its field on the right at one width (as in Linear); names save quietly when the field loses focus (clearing the first name puts the saved one back; a failed save reverts with a toast), and the email field is disabled. **Channels** (the ways the user reaches Winston; an email address for Winston would join Telegram here): a row like a connected account, Telegram with the linked account's display name (or @username) under it, a Connected (or Not connected) badge and an ⋯ menu (`IconButton` and `Menu`) with **Change account** and **Disconnect**. Change account, and Connect when nothing's linked, open a dialog with two tabs, Scan (the QR code) and Open here (a button that opens the bot; phones start on it), which closes by itself once a chat is linked (each user has one Telegram chat, so a new one replaces it). Disconnect asks first, then unlinks the chat (`unlinkTelegram`), so Winston can't message the user until they connect again. **Account**: Sign out and Delete account (the confirmation explains what goes, so the row doesn't). The time zone isn't shown or set here: the browser sets it (below).
  - **One path for profile changes:** `updateProfile` (`@winston/db/profile`) serves the site (source `site`), the browser's time zone (`browser`) and the CLI (`winston`). It stores time zones by their canonical IANA name (`canonicalTimeZone` in `@winston/shared/time`, now the only zone check) and trims names (a first name is required, a last name may be blank). Each field that actually changes records `system.settings.changed` with `field`, `old`, `new` and `source`; an unchanged value records nothing. A time-zone change also reschedules the user's cron schedules in the new zone.
  - **Following the device:** once per app load, the `_authed` layout compares the browser's zone with **the one it last reported** (`users.browser_timezone`) and, if they differ, reports it (`syncBrowserTimezone` → `followBrowserTimezone` in `@winston/db/profile`), which records it and adopts it as the user's zone; a quiet toast says so. Comparing with the last report rather than the saved zone is what lets a zone Winston set stick: a laptop still on home time reports nothing new, while carrying the device somewhere (or back home) does. The toaster is mounted in the root layout.
- **Sidebar width** persists in the `winston_sidebar_width` cookie, written by the browser when a resize ends (it's a preference, not a secret), and read by the server so the page renders at that width without a jump.
- **Dev design view:** "App shell" with default and drawer open (use the Mobile frame). "Home" with every setup and status state, "Profile" (default, its Telegram states and the delete confirmation), "Sign in" with the account-deleted notice, "Connected accounts" empty, with a few accounts, with one expiring and with one expired, and "Account dialog" (over the list) with a toggle saving, saved and failed, unavailable capabilities, an expired calendar, the disconnect confirmation and a disconnected account.

**Public**

| Route         | Purpose                                                                                                                                                              | Notable states                                            |
| ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| `/`           | Sign in with Google (no public homepage for now). Signed-in visitors go to `/home`                                                                                   | not allowlisted, OAuth error                              |
| `/t/<token>`  | Handoff live view (mobile-first, no sidebar)                                                                                                                         | connecting, live, reconnecting, expired/invalid, resolved |
| anything else | Not found (`src/pages/not-found-page.tsx`, the router's `defaultNotFoundComponent`): "Page not found" and **Go to Winston** (to `/`, which leads home or to sign-in) | —                                                         |

**App (sidebar)**

| Sidebar group | Route                         | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Notable states                                                     |
| ------------- | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| —             | `/home`                       | Overview: Winston's status (computer ready, Telegram linked, accounts needing attention). **Doubles as first-run setup:** until everything is connected it shows a setup checklist (computer provisioning, Connect Telegram with button + QR, connect a first account)        | provisioning, failed, setup incomplete, all good, attention needed |
| —             | `/accounts`                   | **Connected accounts** (the sidebar label): the mail and calendar accounts, and **Add account**                                                                                                                                                                               | empty, auth expiring/expired, disconnected                         |
| —             | `/accounts?account=<acct_id>` | One account, in a dialog over the list: capability toggles, reconnect, disconnect                                                                                                                                                                                             | saving, error, disconnect confirmation                             |
| —             | `/profile`                    | First/last name, email (read-only), time zone; the **Telegram** link (status, connect or relink with button + QR); **Sign out**, and **Delete account** (everything: computer, data, tokens) with a confirmation, on the page rather than in the sidebar (the founder's call) | saving, error                                                      |

**Dev only**

| Route         | Purpose                                                                                             |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `/dev/design` | Every page × every state × desktop and mobile widths, side by side. Excluded from production builds |

- **Time zone:** captured from the browser at signup. **When the web app is opened and the browser's zone has changed since it last reported one, the user's zone follows it** (emitting `system.settings.changed`). Winston can also change it with `winston me update --timezone <IANA>` when the user says they're somewhere else, and that sticks until the device itself moves (decided in cca55d: following the device only on a change keeps both working).
- **What Winston may change about the account:** the time zone only (cca55d). Names are the user's to edit on the site, since they identify the account; a nickname or preferred name belongs in Winston's notes. When he changes the zone he says so in his reply, in the new local time.

## 21. Repo bootstrap

- **Workspace:** Bun workspaces (`apps/*`, `packages/*`, `infra`) holding `apps/{api,agents,gateway,web,cli,winstond}` and `packages/{db,domain,shared,prompts,ui,connectors,vm-api}`, plus `infra/` and `image/`. Package scope `@winston/*`. **Packages and directories are created by the ticket that first needs them**, never stubbed ahead of time.
- **Pins via `mise.toml`** (project-local): Bun, Node, Moth, `cloudflared`, Packer, the AWS CLI and Terraform.
- **`./scripts/setup.sh`**: one idempotent command from fresh clone to working repo (check first, then act). It never installs global tools. The numbered list at the top of the script is the source of truth for its steps, and tickets that add a setup requirement extend it.
- **Root scripts:** `dev` (all services + tunnel), `lint`, `format`, `typecheck`, `test`, `db:generate`, `db:migrate`, `db:seed`, `image:build:local`, `image:build:ami`, `infra:synth`, `infra:diff`, `infra:deploy`, `docker:build`, among others (`check`, `deploy`, `prod`, `prod:keys`, the `vm:*` scripts and more; see `package.json`).
- **Local services:** `docker-compose.yml` (Postgres, and the VM container via the `VmProvider`).
- **`packages/ui`:** the design system (Tailwind + Base UI primitives, tokens, components). The web app consumes only this.
- **Moth:** `moth.config.yml` + `.moth/` at the root.

## Decision log

| #   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | Small multi-user system (founder + friends). Google sign-in; one VM per user, provisioned at signup.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | This is for friends as well, so user accounts are needed, but at small scale.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 2   | Google-first signup on a website, then link Telegram through a deep link (`t.me/<bot>?start=<token>`). The token is random, single-use and short-lived (~15 min), within Telegram's 64-char `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` limit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | This is Telegram's standard account-linking mechanism. The user starting the bot also gives the bot permission to message them first.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 3   | Site is a minimal settings console with no behavior-specific settings. _(Layout refined by #61: sidebar app, no settings page.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Behavior should be modular and emergent, not hard-coded.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 4   | Proactivity comes from general building blocks (wake-ups, events, memory, judgment), not dedicated features.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Keeps Winston modular. New behaviors come from prompting and memory, not code.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 5   | Two-tier agents: fast front of house + parallel background agents, each with its own browser. Browser handoff by link, with auto-resume. _("Auto-resume" refined by #7: resume on the user's message.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Speed of interaction is a core requirement. Browser use is a core strength.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| 6   | One shared persistent browser profile per user; one tab per background agent. _(Refined by #23: a window per agent, not a tab.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Persistent logins make Winston feel like he has his own computer. Throwaway browsers would need a handoff on every visit to a site.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 7   | Handoff completion: the user taps **Done** on the live view, or messages Winston in chat ("done"). No auto-detect. _(Was: chat only, no buttons; the founder added Done on 2026-10-02, since switching back to Telegram is a step too many on a phone.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Simplest thing that works.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 8   | Front of house does quick lookups (CLI reads, memory) itself; browser, multi-step and slow work is delegated. _(Superseded by #41: the front of house has full capabilities and decides by expected duration.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Keeps simple questions instant while long work never blocks the chat.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 9   | Memory = FIFO rolling window + files on the VM (Winston decides the layout). No summarization, no dedicated memory store. _(Refined by #40: background runs do summarize.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Avoids an extra summarization LLM. Memory lives on Winston's own computer, in keeping with the product.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 10  | Fully static system prompt; nothing injected. Memory layout on the VM is up to Winston.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Maximizes prompt caching. Keeps Winston modular.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 11  | Triggers = schedules + subscriptions to per-app event catalogs (with agent-written filters). Every trigger starts a background agent run. _("Per-app" → per-domain, #42.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Modular: no hard-coded features. New apps add events, not code. Keeps noise out of the front of house's window.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 12  | All inbound content wrapped in `<system_event>` XML envelopes with timestamps in the user's time zone. Untrusted content is escaped.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Real user input stays distinct from events. Prevents prompt injection through emails and web pages.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 13  | Steering: inbound items are coalesced, with a debounce when idle and injection at step boundaries when busy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Never one reply per message. Lets the user redirect work mid-flight.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 14  | Unsent replies are discarded and regenerated when new input arrives. No streaming; typing indicator instead.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | The user never sees a stale answer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 15  | Two event layers (primitives + derived abstractions) with object-scoped subscriptions. `<app>.<resource>.<event>` naming. _(Naming superseded by #42: `<domain>.<resource>.<event>`.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Keeps events factual and modular while sparing Winston fragile bookkeeping (for example, tracking meeting moves himself).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 16  | Superseded by #68. (Was: agents message the user only through a `send_message` tool.)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Makes "process and stay silent" the natural default. Allows multiple messages per turn.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 17  | Triggers have `max_fires`, `expires_at` and `on_expire`. Not visible on the site.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Enables one-shot and "nothing happened" follow-ups. Users shouldn't have to manage machinery.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 18  | Per-app capability toggles (enforced by the server) + a prompt-level confirm-first norm. No tool-approval machinery. _("Per-app" → per-connection toggles, #19.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Simple for the user. Hard toggles cover prompt injection. Confirming feels like a normal assistant conversation.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 19  | Sign-in is identity only. Connections are separate, many per app (work/personal), each with its own permissions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Real users have several Google accounts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 20  | Google tokens live only on the backend. The VM's CLI is a thin client calling the backend, which enforces permissions and audits calls. _(Request path refined by #58.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The VM is the most exposed component. Makes permissions real, not advisory.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| 21  | One background-agent type for all triggers, on Opus 5.5. OpenRouter as provider, pinned to Anthropic. Front of house on Sonnet 5. Effort fixed per profile: front of house low, background high (#67).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Event runs can turn into big tasks. One model gives one shared cache.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 22  | Inbound files are saved to the VM and also given to the model when it can read them. Voice notes are transcribed. Outbound attachments come from VM file paths.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Files become part of Winston's computer and stay usable in later tasks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 23  | Browser: own function tools over raw CDP on real headful Chrome; a window per agent; per-domain lock; site skill files; mandatory verification; per-tab screencast handoff. _("Own function tools" → `winston browser` CLI commands, #41.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Matches 2026 state of the art (hybrid refs + screenshots + code). Model-agnostic. Best anti-bot posture.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 24  | Jev as a confidence-gated fast path for routine browser steps; Opus 5.5 plans, types, judges and verifies.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Worth testing: potentially large speed gains. Measured per site, and removable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 25  | All on AWS (`us-east-1`), CDK. Per-user always-on `t3a.medium` EC2 created at signup from a launch template. Outbound-only `winstond` websocket, SSM admin, nightly EBS snapshots. Per-user cost tracking.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | One cloud with IaC. US IPs for browsing. ~$24/user/mo. No inbound attack surface.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 26  | TypeScript everywhere on Bun; Bun-workspaces monorepo; CLI and `winstond` compiled to single binaries.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Founder's preference. Shared types across every component. No runtime on the VM.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 27  | Separate AWS account for Winston (`winston-prod`) in an AWS Organization, with SSO. Only two environments: local and production.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Hard isolation from the founder's other projects: blast radius, IAM, cost.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 28  | Backend on ECS Fargate (Bun): `api`, `agents`, `gateway`. Postgres (RDS) as the single source of truth, including the job queue and trigger scheduler. Agents checkpointed after every step. _(Plus `web`, #30.)_                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Long-running and parked tasks survive deploys and crashes. One datastore, transactional.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 29  | Push notifications: Gmail through GCP Pub/Sub, Calendar through `events.watch`, with a ~10-min reconciliation sync. GCP resources in Terraform.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Near-instant events, and the design scales. The GCP project is needed for OAuth anyway.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 30  | Website: TanStack Start on Bun as a `web` Fargate service behind CloudFront, with server functions for the console. `api` (Hono) handles machine-facing endpoints. Google sign-in with Postgres sessions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Founder's preference. Type-safe end to end. Keeps webhooks separate from the user-facing site.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 31  | No observability/eval tooling. Postgres keeps an append-only log of every model call, tool call, Jev decision and prompt version, with binaries in S3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Anything can be reconstructed from the DB when needed. Avoids extra systems.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 32  | The only guardrail is a per-run step cap (~100 model calls).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Simplest limit that stops a stuck agent from silently burning money.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 33  | No onboarding flow. Winston says hello and the user guides him from there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Keep it simple. Behavior grows from conversation.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 34  | Google OAuth app in testing mode. Sign-up and sign-in restricted by an email allowlist in Postgres (initially the founder only).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | No audit needed for a friends-only product. Weekly reconnects are handled by Winston.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 35  | Only the front of house sends messages to the user. Background agents, including event runs, report to it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | One voice. The front of house has context for replies. Multiple results can be merged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 36  | `winston history search` over Postgres full-text search (no embeddings), returning full messages rendered as envelopes. Envelopes are never stored; they are rendered deterministically at read time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Agents rephrase keyword queries well. Structured storage keeps one rendering path for context and search.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

## Risks & flags

- **Event-run cost.** Trigger runs start at `low` and raise their effort when they need to (#71). Watch per-trigger cost in the cost log; if runs often raise their effort, each raise costs a cache miss.
- **Bun under Rosetta.** Running Bun in a `linux/amd64` container on Apple Silicon (Colima with Rosetta) segfaulted during `bun install` (seen 2026-09-27, Bun 1.4.2). The local "VM" image (M2) runs Bun-compiled binaries (`winstond`, the CLI), so building it as amd64 to match the x86 production servers doesn't work locally. **Resolved:** the local image is native `linux/arm64` and production binaries are built for x64 (§18), so provisioning scripts must keep working on both.
- **Datacenter IPs.** AWS IPs are known datacenter ranges. Some sites (ticketing, aggressive Cloudflare setups) may block or challenge Winston despite a real logged-in Chrome. Mitigation: route those domains through a residential proxy, using the browser-backend interface. **Found (2026-10-02, 26dfa2):** plain HTTPS requests from the production VM's AWS address with a Chrome user agent: OpenTable didn't answer at all (connections hung), Ticketmaster, Reddit and Zillow answered 403, Booking.com 202 (a challenge page); Amazon, Google Flights, Airbnb, LinkedIn and Wikipedia answered normally. That's curl, not Chrome (TLS fingerprints differ), so it was an upper bound. **In the VM's real Chrome** (Winston asked by the founder, same day): OpenTable, Zillow and Booking.com load normally; Ticketmaster shows a bot check ("Your Browsing Activity Has Been Paused") and Reddit blocks it ("blocked by network security"). Even from a home connection in the local Docker VM, OpenTable's Akamai answered "Access Denied" to deep links and let searches from its homepage through. So for now only a few sites (ticketing, Reddit) object, and most, including restaurant and travel booking, work from AWS. **Decision (the founder, 2026-10-02):** a bot check is handed to the user (`browser_handoff`), who clears it on the live view; an outright block with nothing to solve is reported. No proxies, CAPTCHA services or fingerprint tricks; routing a few domains through the user's home connection stays an option if blocks become common.
- **Jev access.** TypeSafe's own API is waitlisted, and Jev is a few weeks old with no independent benchmarks. OpenRouter serves it through an _alpha_ decisions endpoint (b782bc), which could change or go away. The browser loop works without it: autopilot reports Jev unavailable and the agent drives directly.
- **Google OAuth verification.** Gmail read scopes are "restricted." An unverified app in _testing_ mode allows up to 100 test users, which covers friends. However, refresh tokens in testing mode expire after **7 days**, so every user would have to re-authorize weekly. The alternative is production verification, which requires a third-party security assessment (CASA) for restricted scopes. **Decided: testing mode**, with Winston-prompted weekly reconnects (see §5, Access control).
- **Workspace (work) accounts.** A Google Workspace admin can block unverified third-party apps from accessing Gmail or Calendar. Connecting a work account may fail depending on the employer's policy. Each connected Google account also has to be on the test-user list while in testing mode.
